## 1. Strong Coupling In A Three-mode Superconducting-spin Hybrid Quantum System

- **Tags**: `ai`  `spectroscopy`  `qubit`  `diamond`  `strong coupling`

### TL;DR

Demonstrates strong coupling in a three-mode hybrid system of a superconducting transmon qubit, a coplanar-waveguide resonator, and an NV center ensemble.

### Motivation

Hybrid quantum systems combining superconducting qubits with spin ensembles are promising for quantum information processing, but achieving strong coupling in multi-mode configurations is challenging.

### Method

The authors fabricate a device with a fixed-frequency coplanar-waveguide resonator, a transmon qubit, and an NV center ensemble, and perform frequency-domain spectroscopy to observe avoided crossings.

### Result

They observe characteristic three-mode avoided crossings, indicating strong coupling between all three components, and extract coupling strengths and system parameters.

### Conclusion

This work demonstrates a viable platform for hybrid quantum systems with strong coupling, enabling future applications in quantum memory and transduction.

*Keywords: strong coupling, hybrid quantum system, superconducting qubit, NV center, circuit QED*

## 2. Quantum Thermodynamics Of The Caldeira-Leggett Model With Non-equilibrium Gaussian Reservoirs

- **Score**: 9.2 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Quantum  |  **Feed**: Quantum
- **Direction**: Information Thermodynamics
- **Published**: 2026-06-15
- **Authors**: Vasco Cavina And Massimiliano Esposito
- **Link**: [https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-06-15-2136/](https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-06-15-2136/)
- **Tags**: `led`  `dissipation`  `thermodynamics`  `noise`  `dynamics`

### TL;DR

A non-equilibrium Caldeira-Leggett model with squeezed and displaced reservoirs is introduced, enabling work extraction, stochastic driving, and a fluctuation theorem for heat statistics.

### Motivation

To extend the Caldeira-Leggett model to non-equilibrium reservoirs, capturing strong coupling effects and providing a versatile framework for quantum thermodynamics.

### Method

The reservoirs are modeled as collections of squeezed and displaced thermal modes; a modified Keldysh contour technique is used to compute full heat statistics beyond the average.

### Result

Strongly displaced/squeezed reservoirs generate effective time dependence (deterministic or stochastic) in the system Hamiltonian, break the fluctuation-dissipation relation, and yield a fluctuation theorem for energy balance.

### Conclusion

The model bridges quantum and classical thermodynamics, showing that non-equilibrium reservoirs can act as work sources and that the second law is reconciled by accounting for the energy used to create initial non-equilibrium conditions.

*Keywords: Caldeira-Leggett model, non-equilibrium reservoirs, quantum thermodynamics, fluctuation theorem, squeezed states*

### TL;DR

This paper presents a gradiometric, fully tunable C-shunted flux qubit design that reduces sensitivity to flux noise while maintaining wide tunability.

### Motivation

Flux qubits suffer from sensitivity to magnetic flux noise, which limits coherence. A gradiometric design can cancel common-mode noise, but previous implementations lacked tunability.

### Method

The authors design and fabricate a C-shunted flux qubit with a gradiometric loop geometry, enabling independent control of the qubit frequency and the flux bias via two separate flux lines.

### Result

They demonstrate coherence times exceeding 10 μs and full tunability of the qubit frequency over several GHz, with reduced sensitivity to flux noise compared to non-gradiometric designs.

### Conclusion

The gradiometric C-shunted flux qubit is a promising platform for scalable quantum computing, combining long coherence with robust noise immunity and tunability.

## 6. Learning Pure Quantum States Almost Without Regret

- **Score**: 8.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: npj Quantum Info  |  **Feed**: npj Quantum Information
- **Direction**: Quantum Information
- **Published**: 2026-06-26
- **Authors**: Josep Lumbreras, Mikhail Terekhov, Marco Tomamichel
- **Link**: [https://www.nature.com/articles/s41534-026-01305-z](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41534-026-01305-z)
- **Tags**: `online learning`  `quantum state tomography`  `regret minimization`  `pure states`  `sample complexity`

### TL;DR

This paper develops a regret-minimizing online learning algorithm for pure quantum states, achieving near-optimal sample complexity.

### Motivation

Learning unknown quantum states from measurements is fundamental to quantum information processing, but existing methods often require many copies or assume adversarial noise. The authors aim to design an algorithm that performs well even in worst-case scenarios.

### Method

They formulate the problem as a sequential game and apply online convex optimization techniques, specifically using the exponential weight algorithm with a novel loss function tailored to pure states.

### Result

The algorithm achieves regret that scales as O(sqrt(T)) with the number of rounds T, matching the information-theoretic lower bound up to logarithmic factors, and requires only O(log d) measurements per round for d-dimensional states.

### Conclusion

The work provides a practical and theoretically optimal approach to learning pure quantum states in an online setting, with implications for quantum tomography and adaptive sensing.

*Keywords: online learning, quantum state tomography, regret minimization, pure states, sample complexity*

- **Tags**: `entanglement`  `Rényi entropy`  `entanglement entropy`  `scalar field theory`  `replica trick`

### TL;DR

The paper shows that odd-order Rényi entropies of interacting scalar fields can be computed as free energies of replicated systems with quadratic inter-replica couplings, enabling calculation from correlation functions.

### Motivation

Entanglement entropy is a key measure in quantum information and many-body physics, but computing it for interacting quantum field theories is challenging. The authors aim to provide a practical method using correlation functions.

### Method

They derive a replica trick where odd-order Rényi entropies are expressed as free energies of N replicas with additional quadratic couplings within the subsystem at the measurement time, allowing computation via two-point correlation functions.

### Result

The method is demonstrated for free scalar fields and an interacting scalar field theory with quartic coupling, showing that the entropy can be obtained from correlation functions without needing full state tomography.

### Conclusion

This approach provides a feasible way to compute entanglement entropy in and out of equilibrium for scalar field theories, bridging quantum information and quantum field theory.

*Keywords: Rényi entropy, entanglement entropy, scalar field theory, replica trick, correlation functions*

## 17. Universal Thermodynamic Uncertainty Relation For Quantum $f$ Divergences

- **Score**: 8.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Phys. Rev. E  |  **Feed**: Physical Review E
- **Direction**: Information Thermodynamics
- **Published**: 2026-06-23
- **Authors**: Domingos S. P. Salazar
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/n2bt-jcm6](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/n2bt-jcm6)
- **Tags**: `ai`  `thermodynamic uncertainty relation`  `Petz f-divergence`  `quantum thermodynamics`  `entropy production`

### TL;DR

This paper derives a universal thermodynamic uncertainty relation for Petz f-divergences, linking distinguishability to thermodynamic cost.

### Motivation

Thermodynamic uncertainty relations (TURs) bound the precision of observables by entropy production, but existing results are limited to specific divergences. This work aims to unify and generalize TURs for a broad class of quantum f-divergences.

### Method

The author proves that any Petz f-divergence (with operator convex f) can be expressed as a positive superposition of quadratic contrasts, using a mixture representation. This representation is then used to derive a TUR that holds for arbitrary quantum processes.

### Result

A universal TUR is obtained: the distinguishability between initial and final states, measured by any Petz f-divergence, is bounded by the entropy production times a factor depending on f. This generalizes known TURs for the quantum relative entropy and the chi-squared divergence.

### Conclusion

The result provides a unified framework for thermodynamic uncertainty relations in quantum systems, with potential applications to quantum metrology and thermodynamics of information processing.

*Keywords: thermodynamic uncertainty relation, Petz f-divergence, quantum thermodynamics, entropy production, distinguishability*

### TL;DR

### Motivation

### Method

Analytical derivation of stochastic Schrödinger equations that describe reverse diffusion in quantum trajectories, using tools from stochastic calculus and quantum measurement theory.

### Result

The authors develop algorithms for phase estimation with advice and prove nearly matching lower bounds using the polynomial method with trigonometric polynomials, as well as reductions from the unitary recurrence time problem.

### Result

They show that a small number of advice copies do not significantly help, and that many copies do not drastically reduce cost. They also derive a tight bound for error probability reduction in basic phase estimation, showing it costs Θ(1/ε) rather than the O(1/√ε) typical in search.

### Conclusion

The work provides a complete characterization of phase estimation with advice, resolving open questions and offering tight bounds that are useful for designing quantum algorithms.

*Keywords: quantum phase estimation, query complexity, advice states, polynomial method, lower bounds*

## 62. Symplectic Rank Of Non-Gaussian Quantum States

- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/1rtk-1jsn](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/1rtk-1jsn)
- **Tags**: `symplectic rank`  `non-Gaussianity`  `continuous-variable quantum information`  `resource theory`  `bosonic quantum states`

### TL;DR

The symplectic rank is introduced as a unified measure of non-Gaussianity in bosonic quantum states, quantifying the minimum resources for state preparation, compression, and measurement.

### Motivation

Non-Gaussian states are essential for quantum advantage in continuous-variable systems, but a unified measure of non-Gaussianity was lacking. The symplectic rank fills this gap by providing an operationally meaningful resource measure.

### Method

The authors define the symplectic rank based on the minimal number of Gaussian unitaries needed to prepare a state from a Gaussian state, and relate it to the state's covariance matrix and Wigner function. They develop theoretical tools to compute it for various states.

### Result

The symplectic rank is shown to be a monotone under Gaussian operations, and it determines the minimal number of non-Gaussian operations required for state preparation, the compression rate for lossless compression, and the number of measurements needed for tomography.

### Conclusion

The symplectic rank is a powerful and practical measure of non-Gaussianity with direct operational interpretations, advancing the resource theory of non-Gaussianity in continuous-variable quantum information.

*Keywords: symplectic rank, non-Gaussianity, continuous-variable quantum information, resource theory, bosonic quantum states*

- **Tags**: `ai`  `qubit`  `perspective`  `fault-tolerance`  `CSS codes`

### TL;DR

This paper proves impossibility results for addressing logical qubits in high-rate CSS codes using local Clifford circuits, and shows that permuting logical qubits via physical permutations is limited by code rate.

### Motivation

Understanding how to implement logical gates on subsets of logical qubits is crucial for fault-tolerant quantum computation, especially with asymptotically good codes.

### Method

The authors use automorphism theory of CSS codes and combinatorial arguments to derive no-go theorems for addressability under fault-tolerant constraints.

### Result

For non-zero rate CSS codes, 1-local Clifford circuits cannot address logical X, Z, H, or S gates to strict subsets; permuting logical qubits via physical permutations is impossible if rate > 1/3 and distance ≥ 3.

### Conclusion

There is a fundamental trade-off between addressability and code efficiency, which must be considered for practical quantum computation.

*Keywords: CSS codes, addressability, logical gates, fault tolerance, automorphisms*

### TL;DR

This paper extends Pearl's counterfactual semantics to quantum causal models, completing the quantum analogue of the ladder of causation.

### Motivation

Counterfactual reasoning is crucial for causal inference, but a quantum generalization was missing. The authors aim to provide a formal framework for counterfactuals in quantum causal models.

### Method

They define quantum structural causal models (QSMs) and a three-step procedure of abduction, action, and prediction, generalizing Pearl's classical approach.

### Result

Every classical structural causal model can be extended to a QSM, and counterfactual queries agree with classical ones while being more expressive. Quantum counterfactuals allow counterfactual dependence without causal dependence.

### Conclusion

Variational quantum algorithms suffer from barren plateaus, where gradients vanish exponentially with system size. Understanding how noise affects this phenomenon is crucial for practical quantum computing.

The authors derive concentration bounds for the loss function in noisy parametrized quantum circuits, relating the variance of the loss to the circuit's expressivity and noise level.

This paper improves the convergence rate of the quantum Zeno effect to arbitrarily high order using a multi-product formula, with applications to projected dynamics and Bang-Bang decoupling.

### Motivation

The standard quantum Zeno effect has limited convergence rate (order 1), which restricts its efficiency in approximating projected dynamics. Achieving higher-order convergence would enable more accurate simulations and control.

The authors combine a modified Chernoff lemma, an adapted Dunford-Segal approximation, holomorphic functional calculus, and Chebyshev interpolation to construct a multi-product formula that yields arbitrarily high-order convergence.

### Result

They prove convergence of order k for any integer k, and illustrate the method with the bosonic cat code and Bang-Bang decoupling, demonstrating improved approximation of expectation values.

The multi-product Zeno effect provides a systematic way to enhance the convergence rate of Zeno-based approximations, with potential applications in quantum simulation and control.

This paper characterizes optimal probe states for multiparameter estimation of SU(2) and SU(1,1) unitaries in a two-bosonic-mode system, achieving Heisenberg scaling for multiple parameters.

### Motivation

Multiparameter quantum estimation is crucial for quantum metrology, but achieving simultaneous Heisenberg scaling for multiple parameters is challenging. The authors aim to identify resource states that enable such scaling for SU(2) and SU(1,1) unitaries.

### Method

The authors analyze the precision scaling in terms of total particle number, considering both optimal states in fixed-number subspaces and practical measurements restricted to first two moments of generators. They examine states like twin-Fock and two-mode squeezed states.

### Result

Eigenstates of J_z for SU(2) and analogous states for SU(1,1) allow simultaneous Heisenberg scaling for all three parameters. Under restricted measurements, twin-Fock state enables Heisenberg scaling for two parameters, and two-mode squeezed state allows it for a two-parameter SU(1,1) estimation.

The work extends the understanding of multiparameter estimation to non-compact groups and identifies practical states for achieving Heisenberg scaling, with implications for quantum metrology and interferometry.

## 159. Query And Depth Upper Bounds For Quantum Unitaries Via Grover Search

- **Score**: 5.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: Quantum  |  **Feed**: Quantum
- **Direction**: Quantum Information
- **Published**: 2026-06-30
- **Authors**: Gregory Rosenthal
- **Link**: [https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-06-30-2144/](https://quantum-journal.org/papers/q-2026-06-30-2144/)
- **Tags**: `ai`  `qubit`  `Grover search`  `quantum circuit depth`  `query complexity`

### TL;DR

This paper proves upper bounds on query complexity and circuit depth for implementing arbitrary quantum unitaries using Grover search, with matching lower bounds for certain implementations.

### Motivation

Understanding the resources required to implement arbitrary quantum operations is fundamental to quantum computing. This work aims to establish tight bounds on query complexity and circuit depth for general unitaries.

### Method

The authors reduce the problem of implementing a unitary to Grover search, using amplitude amplification techniques. For exact implementation, they also construct arbitrary quantum states with linear depth and then extend to unitaries.

### Result

They show that any n-qubit unitary can be implemented approximately in time O(2^{n/2}) with query access to a classical oracle, and exactly by a circuit of depth O(2^{n/2}) with one- and two-qubit gates and O(2^{n/2}) ancillae. They also prove a matching Ω(2^{n/2}) lower bound for a class of implementations.

### Conclusion

The results provide nearly optimal bounds for implementing arbitrary unitaries via Grover search, highlighting the exponential cost of general unitary synthesis.

*Keywords: Grover search, quantum circuit depth, query complexity, unitary synthesis, amplitude amplification*

---

- **Tags**: `ai`  `simulation`  `correlations`  `noise`  `aging`

### TL;DR

A temporal coarse-graining method for simulating classical stochastic noise in quantum systems, focusing on 1/f noise in solid-state quantum processors.

### Motivation

Simulating quantum systems with temporally correlated classical noise (e.g., 1/f noise) is computationally challenging due to multiple time scales.

### Method

The noise is modeled as a sum of Ornstein-Uhlenbeck processes; temporal coarse-graining is achieved by conditioning on a coarse realization and analytically averaging over bridge processes.

### Result

The method yields deterministic components that capture coarse noise and independent bridge processes with analytic correlators, enabling precomputation of noise propagators.

### Conclusion

The approach offers practical advantages like a simple concatenation rule and is demonstrated with numerical examples.

*Keywords: temporal coarse-graining, Ornstein-Uhlenbeck process, classical stochastic noise, quantum simulation, 1/f noise*

### TL;DR

This paper presents methods to simulate magic state cultivation circuits using a sum of Clifford ZX-diagrams, achieving a significant reduction in the number of terms compared to full stabiliser decomposition.

### Motivation

Magic state cultivation is a key technique for fault-tolerant quantum computing, but simulating these circuits is computationally expensive due to the high non-Clifford gate count. The authors aim to reduce simulation cost by exploiting structure in the circuits.

### Method

The authors develop stabiliser decompositions that represent the non-Clifford state as a sum of Clifford ZX-diagrams, using cutting techniques to reduce the number of terms. They also provide optimisations for numerical simulation.

### Result

They achieve a reduction in the number of terms by more than a factor of 1000 compared to naive decomposition, and demonstrate simulation speeds of nearly 1000 shots per second on a laptop for small circuits.

### Conclusion

The work sheds light on the simulability of high T-count quantum circuits with internal structure, and the methods are useful for simulating the escape stage of magic state cultivation in larger Clifford circuits.

*Keywords: magic state cultivation, stabiliser decomposition, Clifford ZX-diagrams, quantum circuit simulation, non-Clifford gates*

### Motivation

Single-ion optical clocks require long averaging times to reach statistical uncertainty, limiting their practical use. The authors aim to reduce this time by extending probe duration while mitigating time-dilation shifts.

The clock achieves a fractional frequency instability of 1.5e-15 at 1 second and reaches a statistical uncertainty of 1e-18 in under 1000 seconds, while maintaining systematic uncertainty below 1e-18.

### TL;DR

This paper studies how internal parameters of active Brownian particles affect rectified transport in asymmetric ratchets, using a macroscopic experimental model.

Most existing work on rectified transport focuses on structural boundaries; this work shifts to internal properties of particles.

### Method

A vibration-driven vehicle (VDV) serves as a macroscopic model for active Brownian particles; mass and center of mass are systematically varied, and coarse-grained simulations reproduce experimental trends.

### Result

Increasing mass weakens average velocity and persistence length, leading to a noise-dominated localized state; shifting the center of mass induces a non-monotonic reentrant phase transition in rectification probability.

To provide a rigorous derivation of the modified Langevin noise formalism (MLNF) from canonical quantization of macroscopic electromagnetism (CQME) by finding the explicit functional dependence of polariton operators on canonical field operators.

### Method

The authors adopt analytical expressions for polariton operators as constitutive definitions, prove they satisfy bosonic commutation relations from canonical ones, and show they diagonalize the CQME Hamiltonian in the Schrödinger picture.

### Conclusion

The MLNF is fully justified from first principles, providing a solid foundation for modeling quantized electromagnetic fields interacting with lossy media.

### TL;DR

This paper studies the Kondo effect and quantum criticality in a heavy-fermion ferromagnet using spectroscopic techniques.

### Motivation

To understand the role of cerium's 4f electron in pressure-induced quantum criticality in CeRh6Ge4.

### Method

Combined core-level photoemission and polarized x-ray absorption spectroscopy with single-impurity Anderson model simulations.

### Result

Kondo hybridization admixes higher-lying crystal field states into a multiorbital ground state with 4f occupancy ~0.9, explaining the dynamical spin response.

### Conclusion

Kondo hybridization and multiorbital ground state are essential for modeling quantum criticality in this system.

*Keywords: Kondo effect, quantum criticality, heavy-fermion, x-ray absorption spectroscopy, Anderson model*

## 409. Deterministic Initiation Of Inner-shell X-ray Lasers By Two-color Raman Driving

- **Score**: 0.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: New J. Phys.  |  **Feed**: New Journal of Physics
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-06-30
- **Authors**: Carles Serrat
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1367-2630/ae7f43](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1367-2630/ae7f43)
- **Tags**: `ai`  `led`  `noise`  `coherence`  `dynamics`

### TL;DR

This paper proposes a deterministic seeding mechanism for inner-shell x-ray lasers using two-color Raman driving, shifting the initiation from stochastic noise to a driven coherence.

### Motivation

Conventional inner-shell x-ray lasers are initiated by amplified spontaneous emission (ASE), leading to stochastic temporal onset and phase. The authors aim to achieve deterministic control over the lasing initiation.

### Method

The authors analyze a regime where a resonant two-color Raman drive induces a core-valence coherence at the lasing frequency, acting as a seed. They use a microscopic density-matrix description coupled to a linear Maxwell-Bloch-Langevin propagation model with calibrated spontaneous-emission noise, and derive an analytical criterion separating noise-dominated ASE from coherence-dominated seeded regimes.

### Result

An explicit analytical criterion is derived, depending on Raman drive amplitude, small-signal gain, gate duration, and noise strength. Numerical integration confirms the predicted crossover. A microscopic evaluation for Ne provides a quantitative mapping to pump intensities.

### Conclusion

The mechanism enables a coherence-dominated operating regime for inner-shell x-ray lasers, shifting the field origin from spontaneous fluctuations to a driven microscopic coherence, though practical implementation requires two-color x-ray fields with well-defined relative phase.

*Keywords: inner-shell x-ray laser, Raman driving, coherence seeding, amplified spontaneous emission, Maxwell-Bloch-Langevin model*

### Motivation

To directly measure the propulsive force of a microscopic eukaryotic swimmer and test the assumption that swimming speed is a proxy for motor force.

### Method

Optical tweezers were used to trap a single Chlamydomonas cell and measure the force exerted by its flagella during swimming.

### Result

The propulsive force was found to be approximately 0.3 pN, and the relationship between swimming speed and force is not linear, indicating that speed is not a direct proxy for force.

### Conclusion

Direct force measurements reveal that swimming kinematics do not accurately reflect motor force output in eukaryotic swimmers, challenging a common assumption in biophysics.

*Keywords: eukaryotic swimmer, propulsive force, optical tweezers, Chlamydomonas, flagella*

- **Tags**: `led`  `collision`  `topologically-protected surface plasma waves`  `collisionless damping`  `non-uniform magnetic field`

### TL;DR

This paper studies topologically-protected surface plasma waves in non-uniformly magnetized plasma columns, focusing on their propagation and collisionless damping.

### Motivation

To extend the theory of topologically-protected surface plasma waves from uniformly magnetized to realistic three-dimensional magnetic fields, and to understand their absorption and reflection properties.

### Method

The authors theoretically analyze wave propagation in a cold plasma column with a non-uniform magnetic field produced by finite-sized coils, using a model that accounts for collisionless damping by upper-hybrid resonances.

### Result

They find that TSPWs are collisionlessly absorbed in a localized evanescent region as they propagate into decreasing magnetic field, with negligible reflection, contrasting with unmagnetized non-uniform plasmas.

### Conclusion

The study demonstrates robust, reflection-free absorption of topologically-protected surface waves in realistic non-uniform magnetic fields, which may have implications for plasma heating or wave manipulation.

- **Tags**: `ai`  `collision`  `Higgs boson`  `exotic decays`  `pseudoscalar`

### TL;DR

This paper reports an ATLAS search for exotic Higgs boson decays into pseudoscalars decaying to tau leptons, with no significant excess observed.

### Motivation

To probe beyond Standard Model physics through exotic Higgs decays, specifically into four tau leptons via intermediate pseudoscalars.

### Method

Analysis of 140 fb^-1 of pp collision data at 13 TeV from the ATLAS detector, selecting events with hadronic and leptonic tau decays and performing a statistical interpretation.

### Result

No significant excess over Standard Model background; 95% CL upper limits on the branching ratio are set between 0.06 and 0.23 for pseudoscalar masses 15-60 GeV.

### Conclusion

The search constrains exotic Higgs decays to four tau leptons, but does not find evidence for new physics.

*Keywords: Higgs boson, exotic decays, pseudoscalar, tau leptons, ATLAS*

## 523. Orthogonal Phase Modulation And Lissajous Mode Decoupling In Light-induced Thermoelastic Spectroscopy For Real-time Multi-component Gas Sensing

- **Score**: 0.0 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: Rep. Prog. Phys.  |  **Feed**: Reports on Progress in Physics (ROPP)
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-06-18
- **Authors**: Hanxu Ma, Shunda Qiao, Ying He, Haiyue Sun And Yufei Ma
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae79a3](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae79a3)
- **Tags**: `led`  `noise`  `spectroscopy`  `bot`  `experiment`

### TL;DR

This paper presents a novel optical gas sensing technique using orthogonal phase modulation and Lissajous mode decoupling in light-induced thermoelastic spectroscopy for real-time multi-component gas detection.

### Motivation

Real-time multi-component gas sensing is important for environmental monitoring and industrial safety, but existing methods suffer from channel crosstalk and complexity.

### Method

The authors use two continuous-wave lasers modulated at the same frequency with a 45° phase difference, co-exciting a quartz tuning fork. The piezoelectric signal is orthogonally demodulated to decouple the gas concentration information.

### Result

The sensor achieves linear responses for methane and acetylene with low systematic errors (0.54% and 0.79%) and detection limits of 0.32 ppm and 0.29 ppm, respectively.

### Conclusion

The proposed OPM-LITES sensor provides a simple and efficient solution for real-time multi-component gas sensing with a novel theoretical framework for mode decoupling.

*Keywords: orthogonal phase modulation, Lissajous mode decoupling, light-induced thermoelastic spectroscopy, quartz tuning fork, multi-component gas sensing*

Antibiotic resistance is a growing crisis driven by selective pressure from antibiotics; there is a need for alternative strategies that target resistant bacteria without affecting susceptible ones.

### Method

The authors designed a compound that remains harmless until activated by β-lactamase, an enzyme produced by resistant bacteria, and then acts as a photosensitizer to generate reactive oxygen species upon light exposure.

### Result

The compound selectively accumulates in and kills β-lactamase-expressing bacteria under light, while leaving non-resistant bacteria unharmed.

### Conclusion

This approach offers a potential therapeutic strategy to combat antibiotic resistance by specifically targeting resistant pathogens.

### TL;DR

This paper presents a method for intracellular delivery of full-length antibodies using organ-targeted lipid nanoparticles.

### Motivation

Antibodies are highly selective therapeutics but cannot cross cell membranes, limiting their use to extracellular targets. The goal is to enable targeting of intracellular disease-relevant proteins.

### Method

The authors developed lipid nanoparticles (LNPs) functionalized with targeting ligands to deliver full-length antibodies into specific cell types in vivo.

### Result

The LNPs successfully delivered functional antibodies into cells in targeted organs, demonstrating intracellular antibody activity.

### Conclusion

Organ-targeted LNPs offer a promising platform for expanding antibody therapeutics to intracellular targets.

*Keywords: lipid nanoparticles, intracellular delivery, antibody therapeutics, targeted drug delivery*
